          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Medical gas manufacturers; requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No person shall act as a medical gas manufacturer without first obtaining a license from the board and paying any applicable fee specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Application for a medical gas manufacturer license under this section must be made in a manner specified by the board.</p>
          <p>(c) A license must not be issued or renewed for a medical gas manufacturer unless the applicant agrees to operate in a manner prescribed by federal and state law and according to Minnesota Rules.</p>
          <p>(d) A license must not be issued or renewed for a medical gas manufacturer that is required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located unless the applicant supplies the board with proof of licensure or registration. The board may establish standards for the licensure of a medical gas manufacturer that is not required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located.</p>
          <p>(e) The board must require a separate license for each facility located within the state at which medical gas manufacturing occurs and for each facility located outside of the state at which medical gases that are shipped into the state are manufactured.</p>
          <p>(f) Prior to the issuance of an initial or renewed license for a medical gas manufacturing facility, the board may require the facility to pass an inspection conducted by an authorized representative of the board. In the case of a medical gas manufacturing facility located outside of the state, the board may require the applicant to pay the cost of the inspection, in addition to the license fee in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>, unless the applicant furnishes the board with a report, issued by the appropriate regulatory agency of the state in which the facility is located, of an inspection that has occurred within the 24 months immediately preceding receipt of the license application by the board. The board may deny licensure unless the applicant submits documentation satisfactory to the board that any deficiencies noted in an inspection report have been corrected.</p>
          <p>(g) A duly licensed medical gas manufacturing facility may also wholesale or dispense any medical gas that is manufactured by the licensed facility, or manufactured or wholesaled by another properly licensed medical gas facility, without also obtaining a medical gas wholesaler license or medical gas dispenser registration.</p>
          <p>(h) The filling of a medical gas into a final use container, at the point of use and by liquid to liquid transfer, is permitted as long as the facility used as the base of operations is duly licensed as a medical gas manufacturer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.191.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Medical gas wholesalers; requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No person shall act as a medical gas wholesaler without first obtaining a license from the board and paying any applicable fee specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Application for a medical gas wholesaler license under this section must be made in a manner specified by the board.</p>
          <p>(c) A license must not be issued or renewed for a medical gas wholesaler unless the applicant agrees to operate in a manner prescribed by federal and state law and according to Minnesota Rules.</p>
          <p>(d) A license must not be issued or renewed for a medical gas wholesaler that is required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located unless the applicant supplies the board with proof of licensure or registration. The board may establish standards for the licensure of a medical gas wholesaler that is not required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located.</p>
          <p>(e) The board must require a separate license for each facility located within the state at which medical gas wholesaling occurs and for each facility located outside of the state from which medical gases that are shipped into the state are wholesaled.</p>
          <p>(f) Prior to the issuance of an initial or renewed license for a medical gas wholesaling facility, the board may require the facility to pass an inspection conducted by an authorized representative of the board. In the case of a medical gas wholesaling facility located outside of the state, the board may require the applicant to pay the cost of the inspection, in addition to the license fee in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>, unless the applicant furnishes the board with a report, issued by the appropriate regulatory agency of the state in which the facility is located, of an inspection that has occurred within the 24 months immediately preceding receipt of the license application by the board. The board may deny licensure unless the applicant submits documentation satisfactory to the board that any deficiencies noted in an inspection report have been corrected.</p>
          <p>(g) A duly licensed medical gas wholesaling facility may also dispense any medical gas that is manufactured or wholesaled by another properly licensed medical gas facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.191.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Medical gas dispensers; requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person or establishment not licensed as a pharmacy, practitioner, medical gas manufacturer, or medical gas dispenser must not engage in the dispensing of medical gases without first obtaining a registration from the board and paying the applicable fee specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>. The registration must be displayed in a conspicuous place in the business for which it is issued and expires on the date set by the board.</p>
          <p>(b) Application for a medical gas dispenser registration under this section must be made in a manner specified by the board.</p>
          <p>(c) A registration must not be issued or renewed for a medical gas dispenser located within the state unless the applicant agrees to operate in a manner prescribed by federal and state law and according to the rules adopted by the board. A license must not be issued for a medical gas dispenser located outside of the state unless the applicant agrees to operate in a manner prescribed by federal law and, when dispensing medical gases for residents of this state, the laws of this state and Minnesota Rules.</p>
          <p>(d) A registration must not be issued or renewed for a medical gas dispenser that is required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located unless the applicant supplies the board with proof of the licensure or registration. The board may establish standards for the registration of a medical gas dispenser that is not required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located.</p>
          <p>(e) The board must require a separate registration for each medical gas dispenser located within the state and for each facility located outside of the state from which medical gases are dispensed to residents of this state.</p>
          <p>(f) Prior to the issuance of an initial or renewed registration for a medical gas dispenser, the board may require the medical gas dispenser to pass an inspection conducted by an authorized representative of the board. In the case of a medical gas dispenser located outside of the state, the board may require the applicant to pay the cost of the inspection, in addition to the license fee in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>, unless the applicant furnishes the board with a report, issued by the appropriate regulatory agency of the state in which the facility is located, of an inspection that has occurred within the 24 months immediately preceding receipt of the license application by the board. The board may deny licensure unless the applicant submits documentation satisfactory to the board that any deficiencies noted in an inspection report have been corrected.</p>
          <p>(g) A facility holding a medical gas dispenser registration must not engage in the manufacturing or wholesaling of medical gases, except that a medical gas dispenser may transfer medical gases from one of its duly registered facilities to other duly registered medical gas manufacturing, wholesaling, or dispensing facilities owned or operated by that same company, without requiring a medical gas wholesaler license.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.191.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.4.5.0">2021 c 30 art 4 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.151.20"><b>151.20</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.22.0">1969 c 933 s 22</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.21">
        <h1 class="shn">151.21 SUBSTITUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Except as provided in this section, it shall be unlawful for any pharmacist or pharmacist intern who dispenses prescriptions, drugs, and medicines to substitute an article different from the one ordered, or deviate in any manner from the requirements of a prescription drug order without the approval of the prescriber.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Dispense as written prescription drug orders.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When a pharmacist receives a paper or hard copy prescription drug order on which the prescriber has personally written in handwriting "dispense as written" or "D.A.W.," a prescription sent by electronic transmission on which the prescriber has expressly indicated in a manner consistent with the standards for electronic prescribing under Code of Federal Regulations, title 42, section 423, that the prescription is to be dispensed as transmitted and which bears the prescriber's electronic signature, or an oral prescription for which the prescriber has expressly indicated that the prescription is to be dispensed as communicated, the pharmacist shall dispense the drug as prescribed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Other prescription drug orders.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When a pharmacist receives a paper or hard copy prescription on which the prescriber has not personally written in handwriting "dispense as written" or "D.A.W.," a prescription sent by electronic transmission on which the prescriber has not expressly indicated in a manner consistent with the standards for electronic prescribing under Code of Federal Regulations, title 42, section 423, that the prescription is to be dispensed as transmitted and which bears the prescriber's electronic signature, or an oral prescription in which the prescriber has not expressly indicated that the prescription is to be dispensed as communicated, and there is available in the pharmacist's stock a less expensive generically equivalent drug or, if a biological product is prescribed, a less expensive interchangeable biological product, then the pharmacist shall, after disclosing the substitution to the purchaser, dispense the generically equivalent drug or the interchangeable biological product, unless the purchaser objects. A pharmacist may also substitute pursuant to the oral instructions of the prescriber. A pharmacist may not substitute a generically equivalent drug unless, in the pharmacist's professional judgment, the substituted drug is therapeutically equivalent and interchangeable to the prescribed drug. A pharmacist may not substitute a biological product unless the United States Food and Drug Administration has determined the substituted biological product to be interchangeable with the prescribed biological product. A pharmacist shall notify the purchaser if the pharmacist is dispensing a drug or biological product other than the specific drug or biological product prescribed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Prescriptions by electronic transmission.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Nothing in this section permits a prescriber to maintain "dispense as written" or "D.A.W." as a default on all prescriptions. Prescribers must add the "dispense as written" or "D.A.W." designation to electronic prescriptions individually, as appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Pricing.</span></h2>
          <p>A pharmacist dispensing a drug under the provisions of subdivision 3 shall not dispense a drug of a higher retail price than that of the drug prescribed. If more than one safely interchangeable drug is available in a pharmacist's stock, then the pharmacist shall dispense the least expensive alternative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Sign.</span></h2>
          <p>A pharmacy must post a sign in a conspicuous location and in a typeface easily seen at the counter where prescriptions are dispensed stating: "In order to save you money, this pharmacy will substitute whenever possible an FDA-approved, less expensive, generic drug product, which is therapeutically equivalent to and safely interchangeable with the one prescribed by your doctor, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant, unless you object to this substitution."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section requires a pharmacist to substitute a drug if the substitution will make the transaction ineligible for third-party reimbursement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Disclosure.</span></h2>
          <p>When a pharmacist dispenses a brand name legend drug and, at that time, a less expensive generically equivalent drug or interchangeable biological product is also available in the pharmacist's stock, the pharmacist shall disclose to the purchaser that a generically equivalent drug or interchangeable biological product is available.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Drug formulary.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivision 3 does not apply when a pharmacist is dispensing a prescribed drug to persons covered under a managed health care plan that maintains a mandatory or closed drug formulary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">Coverage by substitution.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When a pharmacist receives a prescription order by paper or hard copy, by electronic transmission, or by oral instruction from the prescriber, in which the prescriber has not expressly indicated that the prescription is to be dispensed as communicated and the drug prescribed is not covered under the purchaser's health plan or prescription drug plan, the pharmacist may dispense a therapeutically equivalent and interchangeable prescribed drug or biological product that is covered under the purchaser's plan, if the pharmacist has a written protocol with the prescriber that outlines the class of drugs of the same generation and designed for the same indication that can be substituted and the required communication between the pharmacist and the prescriber.</p>
          <p>(b) The pharmacist must inform the purchaser if the pharmacist is dispensing a drug or biological product other than the specific drug or biological product prescribed and the reason for the substitution.</p>
          <p>(c) The pharmacist must communicate to the prescriber the name and manufacturer of the substituted drug that was dispensed and the reason for the substitution, in accordance with the written protocol.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">List of excluded products.</span></h2>
          <p>The Drug Formulary Committee established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625">256B.0625, subdivision</a> 13c, shall establish a list of drug products that are to be excluded from this section. This list shall be updated on an annual basis and shall be provided to the board for dissemination to pharmacists licensed in the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Extended supply.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After a patient has obtained an initial 30-day supply of a prescription drug, and the patient returns to the pharmacy to obtain a refill, a pharmacist may dispense up to a 90-day supply of that prescription drug to the patient when the following requirements are met:</p>
          <p>(1) the total quantity of dosage units dispensed by the pharmacist does not exceed the total quantity of dosage units of the remaining refills authorized by the prescriber; and</p>
          <p>(2) the pharmacist is exercising the pharmacist's professional judgment.</p>
          <p>(b) The initial 30-day supply requirement in paragraph (a) is not required if the prescription has previously been filled with a 90-day supply.</p>
          <p>(c) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), a pharmacist may not exceed the number of dosage units authorized by a prescriber for an initial prescription or subsequent refills if:</p>
          <p>(1) the prescriber has specified on the prescription that, due to medical necessity, the pharmacist may not exceed the number of dosage units identified on the prescription; or</p>
          <p>(2) the prescription drug is a controlled substance, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/152.01">152.01</a>, subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.21.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Electronic entry.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within five business days following the dispensing of a biological product, the dispensing pharmacist or the pharmacist's designee shall communicate to the prescriber the name and manufacturer of the biological product dispensed.</p>
          <p>(b) The communication shall be conveyed by making an entry that is electronically accessible to the prescriber through:</p>
          <p>(1) an interoperable electronic medical records system;</p>
          <p>(2) an electronic prescribing technology;</p>
          <p>(3) a pharmacy benefit management system; or</p>
          <p>(4) a pharmacy record.</p>
          <p>(c) Entry into an electronic records system as described in paragraph (b) is presumed to provide notice to the prescriber.</p>
          <p>(d) When electronic communication as specified in paragraph (b) is not possible, the pharmacist or the pharmacist's designee shall communicate to the prescriber the name and manufacturer of the biological product dispensed by using mail, facsimile, telephone, or other secure means of electronic transmission.</p>
          <p>(e) Communication of the name and manufacturer of the biological product dispensed shall not be required if:</p>
          <p>(1) there is no United States Food and Drug Administration-approved interchangeable biological product for the product prescribed; or</p>
          <p>(2) a prescription is being refilled and the biological product being dispensed is the same product dispensed on the prior filling of the prescription.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-22</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.22.0">1937 c 354 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.10.0">1969 c 933 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/101/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 101 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/345/#laws.5.10.0">1993 c 345 art 5 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.8.48.0">1994 c 625 art 8 s 48</a>,49; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.40.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.125.0">2007 c 123 s 125</a>-128; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/122/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 122 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/84/#laws.0.4.0">2017 c 84 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/39/#laws.0.18.0">2019 c 39 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.54.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.4.75.0">2020 c 115 art 4 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/58/#laws.0.89.0">2022 c 58 s 89</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.211">
        <h1 class="shn">151.211 RECORDS OF PRESCRIPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.211.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Retention of prescription drug orders.</span></h2>
          <p>All prescription drug orders shall be kept on file at the location from which dispensing of the ordered drug occurs for a period of at least two years. Prescription drug orders that are electronically prescribed must be kept on file in the format in which they were originally received. Written or printed prescription drug orders and verbal prescription drug orders reduced to writing, must be kept on file as received or transcribed, except that such orders may be kept in an electronic format as allowed by the board. Electronic systems used to process and store prescription drug orders must be compliant with the requirements of this chapter and the rules of the board. Prescription drug orders that are stored in an electronic format, as permitted by this subdivision, may be kept on file at a remote location provided that they are readily and securely accessible from the location at which dispensing of the ordered drug occurred.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.211.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Refill requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 3, a prescription drug order may be refilled only with the written, electronic, or verbal consent of the prescriber and in accordance with the requirements of this chapter, the rules of the board, and where applicable, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/152.11">152.11</a>. The date of such refill must be recorded and initialed upon the original prescription drug order, or within the electronically maintained record of the original prescription drug order, by the pharmacist, pharmacist intern, or practitioner who refills the prescription.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.211.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Emergency prescription refills.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A pharmacist may, using sound professional judgment and in accordance with accepted standards of practice, dispense a legend drug without a current prescription drug order from a licensed practitioner if all of the following conditions are met:</p>
          <p>(1) the patient has been compliant with taking the medication and has consistently had the drug filled or refilled as demonstrated by records maintained by the pharmacy;</p>
          <p>(2) the pharmacy from which the legend drug is dispensed has a record of a prescription drug order for the drug in the name of the patient who is requesting it, but the prescription drug order does not provide for a refill or the time during which the refills were valid has elapsed;</p>
          <p>(3) the pharmacist has tried but is unable to contact the practitioner who issued the prescription drug order, or another practitioner responsible for the patient's care, to obtain authorization to refill the prescription;</p>
          <p>(4) the drug is essential to sustain the life of the patient or to continue therapy for a chronic condition;</p>
          <p>(5) failure to dispense the drug to the patient would result in harm to the health of the patient; and</p>
          <p>(6) the drug is not a controlled substance listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/152.02">152.02, subdivisions 3</a> to 6, except for a controlled substance that has been specifically prescribed to treat a seizure disorder, in which case the pharmacist may dispense up to a 72-hour supply.</p>
          <p>(b) If the conditions in paragraph (a) are met, the amount of the drug dispensed by the pharmacist to the patient must not exceed a 30-day supply, or the quantity originally prescribed, whichever is less, except as provided for controlled substances in paragraph (a), clause (6). If the standard unit of dispensing for the drug exceeds a 30-day supply, the amount of the drug dispensed or sold must not exceed the standard unit of dispensing.</p>
          <p>(c) A pharmacist shall not dispense or sell the same drug to the same patient, as provided in this section, more than one time in any 12-month period.</p>
          <p>(d) A pharmacist must notify the practitioner who issued the prescription drug order not later than 72 hours after the drug is sold or dispensed. The pharmacist must request and receive authorization before any additional refills may be dispensed. If the practitioner declines to provide authorization for additional refills, the pharmacist must inform the patient of that fact.</p>
          <p>(e) The record of a drug sold or dispensed under this section shall be maintained in the same manner required for prescription drug orders under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.211.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.11.0">1969 c 933 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/639/#laws.0.6.0">1973 c 639 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/550/#laws.0.12.0">1988 c 550 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.5.8.0">2014 c 291 art 5 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.9.5.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 9 s 5</a>,6</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.212">
        <h1 class="shn">151.212 LABEL OF PRESCRIPTION DRUG CONTAINERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.212.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prescription drugs.</span></h2>
          <p>Drugs dispensed pursuant to a prescription shall bear a label permanently affixed to the immediate container in which the drug is dispensed and which is received by the purchaser. The label shall bear the name of the manufacturer or distributor of the finished dosage form of the drug and all other information required by law and by rules of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.212.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Controlled substances.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to the requirements of subdivision 1, when the use of any drug containing a controlled substance, as defined in chapter 152, or any other drug determined by the board, either alone or in conjunction with alcoholic beverages, may impair the ability of the user to operate a motor vehicle, the board shall require by rule that notice be prominently set forth on the label or container. Rules promulgated by the board shall specify exemptions from this requirement when there is evidence that the user will not operate a motor vehicle while using the drug.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the requirements of subdivision 1, whenever a prescription drug containing an opiate is dispensed to a patient for outpatient use, the pharmacy or practitioner dispensing the drug must prominently display on the label or container a notice that states "Caution: Opioid. Risk of overdose and addiction."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.212.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Veterinary drugs.</span></h2>
          <p>Drugs dispensed, sold, or distributed in any manner pursuant to the order of a licensed veterinarian shall bear a label permanently affixed to the container in which the drug is dispensed and which is received by the purchaser. The label shall bear the name of the manufacturer or distributor of the finished dosage form of the drug and all other information required by law and the rules of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.212.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Accessible prescription drug container labels.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A pharmacy must:</p>
          <p>(1) make reasonable efforts to inform the public that an accessible prescription drug container label is available at no additional cost, upon request of the patient or the patient's representative, to any patient who has difficulty seeing or reading standard printed labels on prescription drug containers; and</p>
          <p>(2) if the pharmacy knows that the patient has difficulty seeing or reading standard printed labels on prescription drug containers, inform a patient that an accessible prescription drug container label is available at no additional cost upon request of the patient or the patient's representative.</p>
          <p>(b) Subject to paragraph (e), if a patient requests an accessible container label, the pharmacy must provide the patient with a prescription drug container label in large print, Braille, or may provide any other method included in the best practices for access to prescription drug labeling information by the United States Access Board, or its successor organization, depending on the need and preference of the patient. The pharmacy must make reasonable efforts to ensure patient safety and access during the time it takes to provide the requested method of accessibility.</p>
          <p>(c) The accessible container label must:</p>
          <p>(1) be affixed on the container in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.212#stat.151.212.1">151.212, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) last for at least the duration of the prescription;</p>
          <p>(3) contain the information required under subdivisions 1 and 2;</p>
          <p>(4) be available in a timely manner relative to the industry standard time required to produce the accessible container label; and</p>
          <p>(5) conform with the best practices established by the United States Access Board, or its successor organization, for large print and Braille accessible container labels.</p>
          <p>(d) By January 1, 2025, the commissioner of health must publish a list of pharmacies that have informed the commissioner that the pharmacy has the technological capacity to provide an accessible container label to a patient in the timely manner required by paragraph (c), clause (4). The commissioner must update this list on a quarterly basis until January 1, 2026.</p>
          <p>(e) Until January 1, 2026, if the pharmacy does not have the technological capacity to provide an accessible container label to a patient in the timely manner required by paragraph (c), clause (4), the pharmacy is not required to provide an accessible container label to a patient requesting such a label, but the pharmacy must inform the patient of the list of pharmacies with such capacity required pursuant to paragraph (d), if such list is published.</p>
          <p>(f) On and after January 1, 2026, all pharmacies must be able to provide an accessible container label in the timely manner required by paragraph (c), clause (4).</p>
          <p>(g) This subdivision does not apply to prescription drugs dispensed and administered by a correctional institution.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.212.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.12.0">1969 c 933 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/101/#laws.0.3.0">1975 c 101 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/356/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 356 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/338/#laws.0.5.0">1976 c 338 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/550/#laws.0.13.0">1988 c 550 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.12.1.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.60.10.0">2024 c 127 art 60 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.213">
        <h1 class="shn">151.213 COPIES OF PRESCRIPTIONS.</h1>
        <p>Prescriptions on file in a pharmacy are not a public record. A person having custody of or access to such prescription orders shall not divulge the contents thereof or provide a copy thereof to anyone except to:</p>
        <p>(1) the patient for whom the prescription was issued, the patient's agent, or another pharmacist acting on behalf of the patient or the patient's agent;</p>
        <p>(2) the licensed practitioner who issued the prescription;</p>
        <p>(3) the licensed practitioner who is then treating the patient;</p>
        <p>(4) a member, inspector, or investigator of the board or any federal, state, county, or municipal officer whose duty it is to enforce the laws of this state or the United States relating to drugs and who is engaged in a specific investigation involving a designated person or drug;</p>
        <p>(5) an agency of government charged with the responsibility of providing medical care for the patient;</p>
        <p>(6) an insurance carrier or attorney on receipt of written authorization signed by the patient or the patient's legal representative, authorizing the release of such information;</p>
        <p>(7) any person duly authorized by a court order.</p>
        <p>Such copies furnished shall bear on the face thereof the statement "Copy for information only," and may be filed to account for the dispensing of a drug only if such dispensing is authorized in writing or orally by the prescriber and communicated to the pharmacist dispensing and filing such copy.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.213.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.13.0">1969 c 933 s 13</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.214">
        <h1 class="shn">151.214 PAYMENT DISCLOSURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.214.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Explanation of pharmacy benefits.</span></h2>
          <p>A pharmacist licensed under this chapter must provide to a patient, for each prescription dispensed where part or all of the cost of the prescription is being paid or reimbursed by an employer-sponsored plan or health plan company, or its contracted pharmacy benefit manager, the patient's co-payment amount and the pharmacy's own usual and customary price of the prescription or the amount the pharmacy will be paid for the prescription drug by the patient's employer-sponsored plan or health plan company, or its contracted pharmacy benefit manager.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.214.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/39/#laws.0.23.0">2019 c 39 s 23</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.214.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/268/#laws.0.13.0">2004 c 268 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/288/#laws.3.5.0">2004 c 288 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.1.82.0">2005 c 10 art 1 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/267/#laws.1.6.0">2006 c 267 art 1 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.215">
        <h1 class="shn">151.215 CERTIFICATION.</h1>
        <p class="in">A pharmacist must certify a prescription, in compliance with Minnesota Board of Pharmacy rules, before the prescription is dispensed, delivered, mailed, or shipped to a patient or a patient's caregiver. However, if the prescription has been certified by a pharmacist at a licensed central service pharmacy, in compliance with Minnesota Board of Pharmacy rules, an additional certification is not required at the pharmacy that dispenses, mails, or ships the completed prescription to the patient.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.215.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/103/#laws.0.2.0">2007 c 103 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.22">
        <h1 class="shn">151.22 LIABILITY FOR QUALITY OF DRUGS.</h1>
        <p>Every pharmacist in charge or proprietor of a pharmacy shall be responsible for the quality of all drugs, medicines, chemicals, and poisons procured for use and sold therein, except proprietary medicines or other articles sold in the original package of the manufacturer.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.22.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-23</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.23.0">1937 c 354 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.14.0">1969 c 933 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.23">
        <h1 class="shn">151.23 POISONS MUST BE LABELED.</h1>
        <p>It shall be unlawful for any person to sell at retail any poison without affixing to the package or receptacle containing the same a label conspicuously bearing the word "poison," and the name and the business address of the seller, and being satisfied that such poison is to be legitimately used. This section shall not apply to the sale of poison on a physician's written prescription or in the original package of the manufacturer.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.23.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-24</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.24.0">1937 c 354 s 24</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.24">
        <h1 class="shn">151.24 SALE OF POISONS MUST BE RECORDED.</h1>
        <p>It shall be unlawful:</p>
        <p>(1) for any person, either acting independently or while in the employ of another, to sell or give away any poison, as designated by the board, without first recording in a book to be kept for that purpose with an indelible pencil or ink the date, the name and address of the person to whom, and the amount and kind of poison, delivered, except when such poison is sold on the written prescription of a physician;</p>
        <p>(2) to give a false name to be recorded;</p>
        <p>(3) for any person having custody of any such record book to refuse to produce it on demand for the inspection of any authorized agent of the board or other duly authorized officer.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.24.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-25</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.25.0">1937 c 354 s 25</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.151.25"><b>151.25</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.10.13.0">2013 c 108 art 10 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.252">
        <h1 class="shn">151.252 LICENSING OF DRUG MANUFACTURERS; FEES; PROHIBITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.252.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) No person shall act as a drug manufacturer without first obtaining a license from the board and paying any applicable fee specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the license required under paragraph (a), each manufacturer required to pay the registration fee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.066">151.066</a> must pay the fee by June 1 of each year, beginning June 1, 2020. In the event of a change of ownership of the manufacturer, the new owner must pay the registration fee specified under section 151.066, subdivision 3, that the original owner would have been assessed had the original owner retained ownership. The registration fee collected under this paragraph shall be deposited in the opiate epidemic response fund established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.043">256.043</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Application for a drug manufacturer license under this section shall be made in a manner specified by the board.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) No license shall be issued or renewed for a drug manufacturer unless the applicant agrees to operate in a manner prescribed by federal and state law and according to Minnesota Rules.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) No license shall be issued or renewed for a drug manufacturer that is required to be registered pursuant to United States Code, title 21, section 360, unless the applicant supplies the board with proof of registration. The board may establish by rule the standards for licensure of drug manufacturers that are not required to be registered under United States Code, title 21, section 360.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) No license shall be issued or renewed for a drug manufacturer that is required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located unless the applicant supplies the board with proof of licensure or registration. The board may establish, by rule, standards for the licensure of a drug manufacturer that is not required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) The board shall require a separate license for each facility located within the state at which drug manufacturing occurs and for each facility located outside of the state at which drugs that are shipped into the state are manufactured, except a manufacturer of opiate-containing controlled substances shall not be required to pay the fee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065#stat.151.065.1">151.065, subdivision 1</a>, clause (16), or subdivision 3, clause (14), for more than one facility.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) Prior to the issuance of an initial or renewed license for a drug manufacturing facility, the board may require the facility to pass a current good manufacturing practices inspection conducted by an authorized representative of the board. In the case of a drug manufacturing facility located outside of the state, the board may require the applicant to pay the cost of the inspection, in addition to the license fee in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>, unless the applicant furnishes the board with a report, issued by the appropriate regulatory agency of the state in which the facility is located or by the United States Food and Drug Administration, of an inspection that has occurred within the 24 months immediately preceding receipt of the license application by the board. The board may deny licensure unless the applicant submits documentation satisfactory to the board that any deficiencies noted in an inspection report have been corrected.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.252.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Outsourcing facility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No person shall act as an outsourcing facility without first obtaining a license from the board and paying any applicable manufacturer licensing fee specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Application for an outsourcing facility license under this section shall be made in a manner specified by the board and may differ from the application required of other drug manufacturers.</p>
          <p>(c) No license shall be issued or renewed for an outsourcing facility unless the applicant agrees to operate in a manner prescribed for outsourcing facilities by federal and state law and according to Minnesota Rules.</p>
          <p>(d) No license shall be issued or renewed for an outsourcing facility unless the applicant supplies the board with proof of such registration by the United States Food and Drug Administration as required by United States Code, title 21, section 353b.</p>
          <p>(e) No license shall be issued or renewed for an outsourcing facility that is required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located unless the applicant supplies the board with proof of such licensure or registration. The board may establish, by rule, standards for the licensure of an outsourcing facility that is not required to be licensed or registered by the state in which it is physically located.</p>
          <p>(f) The board shall require a separate license for each outsourcing facility located within the state and for each outsourcing facility located outside of the state at which drugs that are shipped into the state are prepared.</p>
          <p>(g) The board shall not issue an initial or renewed license for an outsourcing facility unless the facility passes a current good manufacturing practices inspection conducted by an authorized representative of the board. In the case of an outsourcing facility located outside of the state, the board may require the applicant to pay the cost of the inspection, in addition to the license fee in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.065">151.065</a>, unless the applicant furnishes the board with a report, issued by the appropriate regulatory agency of the state in which the facility is located or by the United States Food and Drug Administration, of a current good manufacturing practices inspection that has occurred within the 24 months immediately preceding receipt of the license application by the board. The board may deny licensure unless the applicant submits documentation satisfactory to the board that any deficiencies noted in an inspection report have been corrected.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.252.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">It is unlawful for any person engaged in drug manufacturing to sell legend drugs to anyone located in this state except as provided in this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.252.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Payment to practitioner; reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless prohibited by United States Code, title 42, section 1320a-7h, a drug manufacturer or outsourcing facility shall file with the board an annual report, in a form and on the date prescribed by the board, identifying all payments, honoraria, reimbursement, or other compensation authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.461">151.461</a>, clauses (4) and (5), paid to practitioners in Minnesota during the preceding calendar year. The report shall identify the nature and value of any payments totaling $100 or more to a particular practitioner during the year, and shall identify the practitioner. Reports filed under this subdivision are public data.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.252.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.10.4.0">2013 c 108 art 10 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.5.10.0">2014 c 291 art 5 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/63/#laws.1.6.0">2019 c 63 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.10.36.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 10 s 36</a>-38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.3.11.0">2020 c 115 art 3 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.253">
        <h1 class="shn">151.253 COMPOUNDING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.253.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Exemption from manufacturing licensure requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.252">151.252</a> shall not apply to:</p>
          <p>(1) a practitioner engaged in extemporaneous compounding, anticipatory compounding, or compounding not done pursuant to a prescription drug order when permitted by this chapter or the rules of the board; and</p>
          <p>(2) a pharmacy in which a pharmacist is engaged in extemporaneous compounding, anticipatory compounding, or compounding not done pursuant to a prescription drug order when permitted by this chapter or the rules of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.253.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Compounded drug.</span></h2>
          <p>A drug product may be compounded under this section if a pharmacist or practitioner:</p>
          <p>(1) compounds the drug product using bulk drug substances, as defined in the federal regulations published in Code of Federal Regulations, title 21, section 207.3(a)(4):</p>
          <p>(i) that:</p>
          <p>(A) comply with the standards of an applicable United States Pharmacopoeia or National Formulary monograph, if a monograph exists, and the United States Pharmacopoeia chapter on pharmacy compounding;</p>
          <p>(B) if such a monograph does not exist, are drug substances that are components of drugs approved for use in this country by the United States Food and Drug Administration; or</p>
          <p>(C) if such a monograph does not exist and the drug substance is not a component of a drug approved for use in this country by the United States Food and Drug Administration, that appear on a list developed by the United States Food and Drug Administration through regulations issued by the secretary of the federal Department of Health and Human Services pursuant to section 503A of the Food, Drug and Cosmetic Act under paragraph (d);</p>
          <p>(ii) that are manufactured by an establishment that is registered under section 360 of the federal Food, Drug and Cosmetic Act, including a foreign establishment that is registered under section 360(i) of that act; and</p>
          <p>(iii) that are accompanied by valid certificates of analysis for each bulk drug substance;</p>
          <p>(2) compounds the drug product using ingredients, other than bulk drug substances, that comply with the standards of an applicable United States Pharmacopoeia or National Formulary monograph, if a monograph exists, and the United States Pharmacopoeia chapters on pharmacy compounding;</p>
          <p>(3) does not compound a drug product that appears on a list published by the secretary of the federal Department of Health and Human Services in the Federal Register of drug products that have been withdrawn or removed from the market because such drug products or components of such drug products have been found to be unsafe or not effective;</p>
          <p>(4) does not compound any drug products that are essentially copies of a commercially available drug product; and</p>
          <p>(5) does not compound any drug product that has been identified pursuant to United States Code, title 21, section 353a, as a drug product that presents demonstrable difficulties for compounding that reasonably demonstrate an adverse effect on the safety or effectiveness of that drug product.</p>
          <p>The term "essentially a copy of a commercially available drug product" does not include a drug product in which there is a change, made for an identified individual patient, that produces for that patient a significant difference, as determined by the prescribing practitioner, between the compounded drug and the comparable commercially available drug product.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.253.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>This section shall not apply to:</p>
          <p>(1) compounded positron emission tomography drugs as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.01">151.01</a>, subdivision 38; or</p>
          <p>(2) radiopharmaceuticals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.253.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Emergency veterinary compounding.</span></h2>
          <p>A pharmacist working within a pharmacy licensed by the board in the veterinary pharmacy license category may compound and provide a drug product to a veterinarian without first receiving a patient-specific prescription only when:</p>
          <p>(1) the compounded drug product is needed to treat animals in urgent or emergency situations, meaning where the health of an animal is threatened, or where suffering or death of an animal is likely to result from failure to immediately treat;</p>
          <p>(2) timely access to a compounding pharmacy is not available, as determined by the prescribing veterinarian;</p>
          <p>(3) there is no commercially manufactured drug, approved by the United States Food and Drug Administration, that is suitable for treating the animal, or there is a documented shortage of such drug;</p>
          <p>(4) the compounded drug is to be administered by a veterinarian or a bona fide employee of the veterinarian, or dispensed to a client of a veterinarian in an amount not to exceed what is necessary to treat an animal for a period of ten days;</p>
          <p>(5) the pharmacy has selected the sterile or nonsterile compounding license category, in addition to the veterinary pharmacy licensing category; and</p>
          <p>(6) the pharmacy is appropriately registered by the United States Drug Enforcement Administration when providing compounded products that contain controlled substances.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.253.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.5.9.0">2014 c 291 art 5 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.10.39.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 10 s 39</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.26">
        <h1 class="shn">151.26 EXCEPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.26.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this chapter shall subject a person duly licensed in this state to practice medicine, dentistry, or veterinary medicine, to inspection by the State Board of Pharmacy, nor prevent the person from administering drugs, medicines, chemicals, or poisons in the person's practice, nor prevent a duly licensed practitioner from furnishing to a patient properly packaged and labeled drugs, medicines, chemicals, or poisons as may be considered appropriate in the treatment of such patient; unless the person is engaged in the dispensing, sale, or distribution of drugs and the board provides reasonable notice of an inspection.</p>
          <p>Except for the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.37">151.37</a>, nothing in this chapter applies to or interferes with the dispensing, in its original package and at no charge to the patient, of a legend drug that was packaged by a manufacturer and provided to the dispenser for dispensing as a professional sample. Samples of a controlled substance shall only be dispensed when one of the approved indications for the controlled substance is a seizure disorder and when the sample is prepared and distributed pursuant to Code of Federal Regulations, title 21, part 203, subpart D.</p>
          <p>Nothing in this chapter shall prevent the sale of drugs, medicines, chemicals, or poisons at wholesale to licensed physicians, dentists and veterinarians for use in their practice, nor to hospitals for use therein.</p>
          <p>Nothing in this chapter shall prevent the sale of drugs, chemicals, or poisons either at wholesale or retail for use for commercial purposes, or in the arts, nor interfere with the sale of insecticides, as defined in Minnesota Statutes 1974, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/24.069">24.069</a>, and nothing in this chapter shall prevent the sale of common household preparations and other drugs, chemicals, and poisons sold exclusively for use for nonmedicinal purposes; provided that this exception does not apply to any compound, substance, or derivative that is not approved for human consumption by the United States Food and Drug Administration or specifically permitted for human consumption under Minnesota law, and, when introduced into the body, induces an effect similar to that of a Schedule I or Schedule II controlled substance listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/152.02">152.02, subdivisions 2</a> and 3, or Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/6800.4210">6800.4210</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/6800.4220">6800.4220</a>, regardless of whether the substance is marketed for the purpose of human consumption.</p>
          <p>Nothing in this chapter shall apply to or interfere with the vending or retailing of any nonprescription medicine or drug not otherwise prohibited by statute that is prepackaged, fully prepared by the manufacturer or producer for use by the consumer, and labeled in accordance with the requirements of the state or federal Food and Drug Act; nor to the manufacture, wholesaling, vending, or retailing of flavoring extracts, toilet articles, cosmetics, perfumes, spices, and other commonly used household articles of a chemical nature, for use for nonmedicinal purposes; provided that this exception does not apply to any compound, substance, or derivative that is not approved for human consumption by the United States Food and Drug Administration or specifically permitted for human consumption under Minnesota law, and, when introduced into the body, induces an effect similar to that of a Schedule I or Schedule II controlled substance listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/152.02">152.02</a>, subdivisions 2 and 3, or Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/6800.4210">6800.4210</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/6800.4220">6800.4220</a>, regardless of whether the substance is marketed for the purpose of human consumption. Nothing in this chapter shall prevent the sale of drugs or medicines by licensed pharmacists at a discount to persons over 65 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.26.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/639/#laws.0.11.0">1973 c 639 s 11</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.26.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-27</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.27.0">1937 c 354 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1953/0/76/#laws.0.5.0">1953 c 76 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/627/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 627 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/192/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 192 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/639/#laws.0.8.0">1973 c 639 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/4/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1981 c 4 art 1 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/550/#laws.0.16.0">1988 c 550 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/285/#laws.0.4.0">2014 c 285 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.5.11.0">2014 c 291 art 5 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.27">
        <h1 class="shn">151.27 EXPENSES.</h1>
        <p>The expenses of administering sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.01">151.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.40">151.40</a> shall be paid from the appropriations made to the State Board of Pharmacy.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.27.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-28</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.28.0">1937 c 354 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/638/#laws.0.30.0">1973 c 638 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.92.0">1976 c 222 s 92</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.151.28"><b>151.28</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/550/#laws.0.20.0">1988 c 550 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.29">
        <h1 class="shn">151.29 VIOLATION A MISDEMEANOR.</h1>
        <p>Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter, or rules hereunder, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor, unless otherwise provided.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.29.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-30</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.30.0">1937 c 354 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.30">
        <h1 class="shn">151.30 COUNTY ATTORNEY TO PROSECUTE.</h1>
        <p>It shall be the duty of the county attorney of the county wherein any offense under this chapter is committed to prosecute the offender, except that when offenses hereunder are committed in cities of the first class it shall be the duty of the city attorney thereof to prosecute the offender. Such prosecutor is authorized to examine the books of any manufacturer or wholesale dealer within the state for the purpose of acquiring information to aid in the prosecution.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-31</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.31.0">1937 c 354 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.301">
        <h1 class="shn">151.301 REPORTS TO COMMISSIONER OF HEALTH.</h1>
        <p>(a) The board shall maintain a record of an event that comes to the board's attention that, in the judgment of the board or a committee of the board, qualifies as an adverse health care event under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.7065">144.7065</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) Within 30 days of making a determination under paragraph (a) that an event qualifies as an adverse health care event, the board shall forward to the commissioner of health a report of the event, including the facility involved, the date of the event, and information known to the board regarding the event. The report shall not include any identifying information for any of the health care professionals, facility employees, or patients involved.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.301.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/186/#laws.0.9.0">2004 c 186 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.302">
        <h1 class="shn">151.302 IMMUNITY.</h1>
        <p>Members of the board and persons employed by the board or engaged in maintaining records and making reports regarding adverse health care events are immune from civil liability and criminal prosecution for any actions, transactions, or publications in the execution of or relating to their duties under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.301">151.301</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.302.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/186/#laws.0.10.0">2004 c 186 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.151.31"><b>151.31</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/550/#laws.0.20.0">1988 c 550 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.32">
        <h1 class="shn">151.32 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>The title of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.01">151.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.58">151.58 </a>shall be the "Pharmacy Practice and Wholesale Distribution Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/35">5808-35</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/354/#laws.0.35.0">1937 c 354 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/550/#laws.0.17.0">1988 c 550 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.40.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.10.40.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 10 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.33">
        <h1 class="shn">151.33 CARELESS DISTRIBUTION OF DRUGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.33.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>No person, directly or indirectly, by agent or otherwise, shall scatter, distribute, or give away any samples of any medicine, drugs, or medical compounds, salve, or liniment of any kind unless the same is delivered into the hands of an adult person, or mailed to such persons through the regular mail service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.33.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person violating any provision of this section shall be guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/99">10275</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/99">10276</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1905/0/33/#laws.0.1.0">1905 c 33 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/23/#laws.0.15.0">1971 c 23 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.335">
        <h1 class="shn">151.335 DELIVERY THROUGH COMMON CARRIER; COMPLIANCE WITH TEMPERATURE REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <p>In addition to complying with the requirements of Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/6800.3000">6800.3000</a>, a mail order or specialty pharmacy that employs the United States Postal Service or other common carrier to deliver a filled prescription directly to a patient must ensure that the drug is delivered in compliance with temperature requirements established by the manufacturer of the drug. The pharmacy must develop written policies and procedures that are consistent with United States Pharmacopeia, chapters 1079 and 1118, and with nationally recognized standards issued by standard-setting or accreditation organizations recognized by the board through guidance. The policies and procedures must be provided to the board upon request.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.335.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.5.3.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 5 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.34">
        <h1 class="shn">151.34 PROHIBITED ACTS.</h1>
        <p>It shall be unlawful to:</p>
        <p>(1) manufacture, sell or deliver, hold or offer for sale any drug that is adulterated or misbranded;</p>
        <p>(2) adulterate or misbrand any drug;</p>
        <p>(3) receive in commerce any drug that is adulterated or misbranded, and to deliver or proffer delivery thereof for pay or otherwise;</p>
        <p>(4) refuse to permit entry or inspection, or to permit the taking of a sample, or to permit access to or copying of any record as authorized by this chapter;</p>
        <p>(5) remove or dispose of a detained or embargoed article in violation of this chapter;</p>
        <p>(6) alter, mutilate, destroy, obliterate, or remove the whole or any part of the labeling of, or to do any other act with respect to a drug, if such act is done while such drug is held for sale and results in such drug being adulterated or misbranded;</p>
        <p>(7) use for a person's own advantage or to reveal other than to the board or its authorized representative or to the courts when required in any judicial proceeding under this chapter any information acquired under authority of this chapter concerning any method or process that is a trade secret and entitled to protection;</p>
        <p>(8) use on the labeling of any drug any representation or suggestion that an application with respect to such drug is effective under the federal act or that such drug complies with such provisions;</p>
        <p>(9) in the case of a manufacturer, packer, or distributor offering legend drugs for sale within this state, fail to maintain for transmittal or to transmit, to any practitioner licensed by applicable law to administer such drug who makes written request for information as to such drug, true and correct copies of all printed matter that is required to be included in any package in which that drug is distributed or sold, or such other printed matter as is approved under the federal act. Nothing in this paragraph shall be construed to exempt any person from any labeling requirement imposed by or under provisions of this chapter;</p>
        <p>(10) conduct a pharmacy without a pharmacist in charge;</p>
        <p>(11) dispense a legend drug without first obtaining a valid prescription for that drug;</p>
        <p>(12) conduct a pharmacy without proper registration with the board;</p>
        <p>(13) practice pharmacy without being licensed to do so by the board;</p>
        <p>(14) sell at retail federally restricted medical gases without proper registration with the board except as provided in this chapter; or</p>
        <p>(15) sell any compound, substance, or derivative that is not approved for human consumption by the United States Food and Drug Administration or specifically permitted for human consumption under Minnesota law, and, when introduced into the body, induces an effect similar to that of a Schedule I or Schedule II controlled substance listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/152.02">152.02, subdivisions 2</a> and 3, or Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/6800.4210">6800.4210</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/6800.4220">6800.4220</a>, regardless of whether the substance is marketed for the purpose of human consumption.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.15.0">1969 c 933 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/25/#laws.0.35.0">1971 c 25 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/550/#laws.0.18.0">1988 c 550 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/314/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 314 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/412/#laws.0.4.0">1990 c 412 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/285/#laws.0.5.0">2014 c 285 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.35">
        <h1 class="shn">151.35 DRUGS, ADULTERATION.</h1>
        <p>A drug shall be deemed to be adulterated:</p>
        <p>(1) if it consists in whole or in part of any filthy, putrid or decomposed substance; or if it has been produced, prepared, packed, or held under unsanitary conditions whereby it may have been rendered injurious to health, or whereby it may have been contaminated with filth; or if the methods used in, or the facilities or controls used for, its manufacture, processing, packing, or holding do not conform to or are not operated or administered in conformity with current good manufacturing practice as required under the federal act to assure that such drug is safe and has the identity, strength, quality, and purity characteristics, which it purports or is represented to possess; or the facility in which it was produced was not registered by the United States Food and Drug Administration or licensed by the board; or, its container is composed, in whole or in part, of any poisonous or deleterious substance which may render the contents injurious to health; or it bears or contains, for purposes of coloring only, a color additive which is unsafe within the meaning of the federal act, or it is a color additive, the intended use of which in or on drugs is for the purposes of coloring only, and is unsafe within the meaning of the federal act;</p>
        <p>(2) if it purports to be or is represented as a drug the name of which is recognized in the United States Pharmacopoeia or the National Formulary, and its strength differs from, or its quality or purity falls below, the standard set forth therein. Such determination as to strength, quality, or purity shall be made in accordance with the tests or methods of assay set forth in such compendium, or in the absence of or inadequacy of such tests or methods of assay, those prescribed under authority of the federal act. No drug defined in the United States Pharmacopoeia or the National Formulary shall be deemed to be adulterated under this paragraph because it differs from the standard of strength, quality, or purity therefor set forth in such compendium, if its difference in strength, quality, or purity from such standard is plainly stated on its label;</p>
        <p>(3) if it is not subject to the provisions of paragraph (2) and its strength differs from, or its purity or quality differs from that which it purports or is represented to possess;</p>
        <p>(4) if any substance has been mixed or packed therewith so as to reduce its quality or strength, or substituted wholly or in part therefor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.16.0">1969 c 933 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/285/#laws.0.6.0">2014 c 285 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.36">
        <h1 class="shn">151.36 DRUGS, MISBRANDING.</h1>
        <p>A drug shall be deemed to be misbranded:</p>
        <p>(1) if its labeling is false or misleading in any particular;</p>
        <p>(2) if in package form and not dispensed pursuant to a prescription unless it bears a label containing (a) the name and place of business of the manufacturer, packer, or distributor, (b) a statement of ingredients, and (c) an accurate statement of the net quantity of the contents in terms of weight, measure, or numerical count, provided, however, that under (c) reasonable variations shall be permitted, and exceptions as to small packages shall be allowed in accordance with the federal act;</p>
        <p>(3) if any word, statement, or other information required by or under authority of this chapter to appear on the label or labeling is not prominently placed thereon with such conspicuousness (as compared with other words, statements, designs or devices, in the labeling) and in such terms as to render it to be read and understood by the ordinary individual under customary conditions of purchase and use;</p>
        <p>(4) if it otherwise fails to meet the labeling requirements of the federal act.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.151.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/933/#laws.0.17.0">1969 c 933 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/285/#laws.0.7.0">2014 c 285 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.151.361">
        <h1 class="shn">151.361 MANUFACTURER DISCLOSURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.361.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">After January 1, 1976.</span></h2>
          <p>The manufacturer, packager, or distributor of any human use legend drug sold, delivered, or offered for sale in the state of Minnesota after January 1, 1976 must have printed on the label on the immediate container of the drug the name and address of the manufacturer of the finished dosage form of the drug.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.151.361.2">
